But 0 could be a valid sum. However, in this case, the column has strings, so no numbers, sum would be 0 if we filter numbers? But we need to return empty string, not 0. So we need to return empty string when there are no numeric values in the column.\n\nAlternatively, maybe the requirement is that column_totals should ignore text cells entirely, meaning they are not included in the sum, and if a column has all text cells, the total is empty. So the total row is \"TOTAL,4,6\" where 4 and 6 are the column totals of the data rows. Note that column totals should be computed by column_totals function. But column_totals currently has the issue we need to fix first. However, for the total row, we need to compute totals of the data rows (excluding any existing total row?). In this case, data rows are [[1,2],[3,4]], totals are [4,6]. So we need to compute column totals of the rows argument, then prepend \"TOTAL,\" to the joined string of totals.\n\nSo export_csv should: if totals=True, compute total_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + column_totals(rows). Then render all rows including the total row? Or append after rendering existing rows.
